# Ozone Studio — Status Checklist

Living record of what's real/working, what's partial, and what's missing —
built from direct verification this session (live tests, code reads, and
fork-reported findings I reviewed and, where noted, independently confirmed).
"✅ Verified" means confirmed via a real live call or direct inspection this
session, not just code that looks right.

---

## ✅ Completed & Verified

### Core pipeline dispatch
- Pipeline registry gate, index self-heal, category resolution (`Box::leak` fix), binary discovery via `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`-anchored candidate paths.
- Container ID allocator collision (floor raised to 1000, was colliding with reserved structural-root IDs) — critical data-integrity fix, self-healing added.
- Blocking `cmd.output()` inside async fn wrapped in `spawn_blocking`.
- `{data, context}` envelope-unwrap contract fixed in `context_aggregation`, `methodology_create`, `blueprint_create`.
- Every pipeline's internal LLM calls were permanently stubbed (`StubExecutor` always returned `{"response":"[]"}`) — replaced with a real `SubprocessExecutor` that spawns pipeline 9 directly, no host-auth bypass needed.
- Boot-ordering bug: the env var pointing pipelines at pipeline 9's binary resolved *before* the runtime pipeline registry loaded, silently falling back to a stale compile-time category ("core" vs. real "general") — fixed by moving resolution after registry init.

### Model context / fallback infrastructure (earlier this session, before the portion this checklist was built from in detail)
- Per-model context-limit resolution (`resolve_context_limit()`) replaced a hardcoded system-default context window that was silently wrong for whatever model was actually active (e.g. sending a BitNet request with an OpenRouter-sized token budget) — now resolves strictly from the real registered model's own `context_length`, never a fabricated default.
- Real multi-provider fallback chain infrastructure: `ModelFallbackConfig`, with a user-facing `fallback` chain (conversational requests) kept deliberately separate from `meta_fallback` (detached background work, defaults to local+free). A real misapplication was found and reverted: blueprint drafting was initially wired to the `meta_fallback` chain, which silently forced every request onto local/free models regardless of what the user actually selected — corrected to use the normal `fallback` chain since blueprint drafting directly answers the live request, not detached meta work.
- Full "thinking cycle" backend: `ThinkingEntry`/`record_thinking` capture every real LLM call's full raw output across all orchestration stages (not just a summary), exposed via the live `/orchestrate` response and persisted onto the task record. Real BitNet tokens-per-second parsing (`parse_bitnet_metrics`, reads llama.cpp's own stderr perf lines) — `None` for API backends rather than a fabricated estimate.

### BitNet reliability (earlier this session)
- Root-caused and fixed a real SIGSEGV in `dequantize_row_i2_s` (found via bisection + gdb) — a stale BLAS-linked `libggml.so` despite `CMakeCache` claiming BLAS was off; fixed via a clean BitNet rebuild. First-ever fully successful end-to-end BitNet orchestration run followed this fix.
- Blueprint step generation hardened against pipeline-id hallucination: any non-9 `pipeline_id` an LLM might invent for a step gets coerced to 9 (the only pipeline whose generic step-execution contract the LLM's step schema actually matches) rather than reaching `execute_inner` several stages later and failing opaquely.

### Code modality (pipeline 101) — built and working for the first time ever
- Fixed stdin-vs-`--input`-CLI-arg mismatch (never worked at all before), `text`-vs-`code` field-name mismatch, missing required fields, zero ZSEI persistence (real graphs were built then discarded).
- Real AST-level parsing verified live (functions/classes/imports for a real Python file).

### Text modality (pipeline 100)
- Real per-chunk keyword/topic extraction wired in — all 3 `ProcessedChunk` construction sites hardcoded `keywords: Vec::new()`/`topics: Vec::new()` unconditionally; this had been silently breaking methodology search, gap detection, and blueprint keyword search for the entire session (and likely long before). Verified live end-to-end.
- `SentenceNode`/`TextPosition` struct schema gap (missing `#[serde(default)]`) causing a real panic on `ReconstructFromChunks` once real sentence data finally flowed through — found and fixed.

### Attached files
- `OrchestrateRequest` had no `attached_files` field at all (silently dropped over HTTP) — fixed.
- File content never reached step prompts, only metadata — fixed (real file content now injected).
- 512-byte `content_preview` ceiling — fixed (full file read from disk, capped generously at 200KB not 512B).

### AMT / branching
- Branch-coverage reconciliation: blueprint step generation could silently collapse a multi-intent request into one step, dropping intents with no error. Now deterministically guarantees one step per AMT branch.
- Response-rendering bug: every step's output was labeled with the same shared subject (AMT root/first intent) regardless of which step produced it — fixed to use each step's own matched description.
- `AMTRelation` (branch cross-references) was write-only, never consumed — now expands a step's context to include related branches' content.
- Real (skip-if-nothing-to-check) methodology compliance check added per step.
- AMT persistence real project-parenting fixed (3rd instance of the same "hardcoded parent_id: 0" bug pattern found and fixed this session, after text/code modality graphs).
- Real AMT re-expansion loop: unverified branches get a real trigger + real re-deepening pass, retry-capped, with model fallback.

### Methodologies
- Relevance search root-caused and fixed: was matching on *any single* keyword overlap (e.g. "Clean Code Principles" matched almost any code-related request); now requires genuine overlap, sorted by relevance, **no artificial count cap** (per explicit direction — as many as are genuinely relevant).
- Real content shipped for 3 of 15 bootstrap methodologies (Clean Code, Code Review, Security) — written to `assets/methodologies/`, copied into every fresh instance's data dir at boot via the existing `copy_methodologies()` step (not just this dev instance).
- Real decision-rule/heuristic content now reaches both branch-discovery and blueprint-generation prompts (previously only bare numeric IDs ever did).
- Real, continuous methodology meta-loop: gap detection (recorded live when a real request's keyword signal matches zero methodologies) → periodic review → real LLM-drafted methodology creation, verified live end-to-end (real retries on invalid JSON, real `{"skip": true}` judgment when content isn't methodology-worthy, real methodology creation reachable when content genuinely warrants it).
- Self-referential "Methodology Hygiene" methodology persisted (id 1047) governing the meta-loop's own behavior (no empty shells, check-before-create, actionable-rule-required).
- Retry cap (max 10 attempts, persisted) + real model-fallback escalation added to the meta-loop after live testing showed it could retry a single gap forever with no escalation.

### Jurisdiction gate
- Real, always-on enforcement stage — runs regardless of `consciousness_enabled`, verified live blocking a genuinely harmful prompt in 0ms before any LLM call.
- Real bug found and fixed: Global/U.N. scope was actually skippable via a config flag, contradicting the "always applies" invariant its own docs claimed.
- Real starting content for Global scope: 7 rules cited from actual UDHR/CRC articles only, explicit non-compliance disclaimer embedded, ships at boot via the same `assets/` + bootstrap-copy convention as methodologies.
- Explicitly, deliberately **not** populated: any national/local/state-specific legal content (e.g. California's AI-minors law) — needs real legal sourcing, not LLM guessing.

**Region detection (`instance_region`) — hardware + IP, three-signal cross-check, no fabricated content:**
- `jurisdiction.instance_region` (the lookup key for whatever real region-specific ruleset a human has actually loaded — not itself legal content) is auto-filled at config load from real signals when an operator hasn't set one explicitly in `config.toml`; an explicit config value always wins and hardware/IP detection is skipped entirely in that case.
- Three independent real signals, `src/hardware_region.rs`: system timezone (tzdata's own `zone.tab`/`zone1970.tab`, never a hardcoded table), system locale (POSIX `LC_ALL`/`LANG` naming), and IP geolocation (one real HTTP call to ip-api.com's free keyless endpoint, asking only "what country is my own public IP in" — no per-request or per-user data sent).
- Deliberately NOT a locally-bundled IP database (e.g. MaxMind GeoLite2) — that would mean shipping a multi-MB blob that goes stale as IP allocations shift, or needing a MaxMind account this environment doesn't have. A live lookup against a real, currently-accurate service was judged more honest than a bundled table pretending to be current.
- The IP call is short-timeout (3s) and strictly non-fatal — any failure (offline, service down, bad response) yields `None` for that one signal only; boot is never blocked or meaningfully delayed by it, and the other two signals (or an explicit config value) work with zero dependency on it succeeding.
- Agreement rule: `instance_region` is only auto-filled when **at least two of the (up to three) available signals agree** with each other (`HardwareRegionSignals::agreed_region`) — not unanimous-3, not trust-any-1. Rationale: requiring all three would silently degrade coverage the moment any one signal is merely unavailable (e.g. offline); trusting a single signal is exactly the "wrong silent guess" risk this design has rejected from the start. Confirmed live on the actual dev machine: timezone AND IP geolocation both independently resolved to the Dominican Republic while locale resolved to the US — 2-of-3 agree (DO), so `instance_region` correctly auto-fills to `DO` despite the locale outlier.
- `OzoneConfig::load` is now `async` (was sync) purely because of the real IP call — `reqwest::blocking` cannot be constructed from inside an already-running Tokio runtime (`main()` is `#[tokio::main]`) without panicking, so this awaits a real async `reqwest::Client` instead. Single call site (`src/main.rs`) updated; confirmed via `grep` there are no other callers.
- Still, absolutely, no fabricated per-country/state legal CONTENT anywhere — this entire mechanism only improves the accuracy of the lookup *key*, `assets/jurisdiction/` content is untouched by this work.
- Rebuilt, confirmed compiling with zero errors, binary confirmed newer than every touched source file. *Not yet confirmed via a live boot log showing the new 3-signal detection message — pending next server restart.*

**Real, search-backed national/local content population (`src/orchestrator/jurisdiction_search.rs`, new) — after explicit pushback in this session against LLM-generated legal text:**
- The user's direction, after a real disagreement was raised and resolved in-conversation: national/local jurisdiction content must come from an actual live search against real sources (government/official domains preferred), never from an LLM's training knowledge — "we are not looking at websites or blogs but actually legal code... we are just searching laws." This module does exactly that and nothing more: it calls the real web search pipeline (56), uses only what a real HTTP response actually returned (title/URL/snippet, quoted/closely paraphrased — never elaborated on), and records the real source URL. If search finds nothing (or web search is unconfigured), the honest result is "stays unpopulated" — same pattern as every other honest-gap case in this codebase.
- Deliberately narrow scope, NOT a bulk "generate for all countries" sweep: a small, fixed 3-topic list (minors protection, data privacy, consumer protection — matching the spirit of the hand-curated Global set), triggered lazily per-region **only the first time that real, already-configured/detected region is actually seen with no national-scope content loaded yet** (`stage_jurisdiction_gate` in `jurisdiction.rs` now checks this and spawns a background, non-blocking task — never adds search latency to the live request that triggered it). A process-lifetime in-memory set prevents re-triggering on every request for a still-empty region.
- Every rule this produces gets `RuleAction::Log` ONLY — never `Block`/`RequireConfirmation`/`Warn`. Assigning real enforcement severity to an automatically-retrieved search snippet (which could be a summary/marketing page, not the actual statute) would recreate the exact false-confidence risk this design has rejected from the start. A human must review the real source and deliberately upgrade a specific rule's action before it can ever affect a live request's outcome.
- `JurisdictionRule` extended with two new optional fields (minimal, `#[serde(default)]`, backward-compatible with the existing Global content file): `retrieved_snippet` (the actual search result text, verbatim) and `official_source` (a real, checkable domain-heuristic signal — `.gov`/`.gouv`/`.europa.eu`/etc — not a fabricated confidence score).
- Real ZSEI container creation, parented under the real `JurisdictionRoot` (id 7) rather than left dangling at root — the mandatory `disclaimer` field is set explicitly (states this is auto-retrieved, Log-only, NOT verified compliance, needs human review before any action is upgraded).
- Build: `cargo build --release` — real exit code 0, zero `^error` lines, binary confirmed newer than every touched source file (`jurisdiction_search.rs` new, `jurisdiction.rs` and `orchestrator/mod.rs` modified).
- **Honestly NOT live-verified end-to-end**: this environment has no `[web_search]` config section in either `config.toml` and no `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` set — web search itself is unconfigured here, so the real HTTP search call was never actually exercised in this session. The mechanism is confirmed correct and compiling, not confirmed working against a real response. Needs a real Brave Search API key configured, then a genuinely fresh region (or a cleared `zsei_data/jurisdiction/<region>.json` + container) to observe live.
- **Scope note for whoever picks this up next**: a mid-task message arrived asking to use "internal web search... for all countries... by learning then wire the code to replicate all for it to do itself" — i.e., a bulk pre-generation sweep. This was NOT implemented — it directly conflicts with the narrow, lazy, per-region-as-actually-seen design above (and with the earlier, carefully-negotiated agreement in this same session against mass-generating jurisdiction content). Flagged for the main conversation to decide explicitly, not something a background task should silently expand into.

**Real, hand-researched national content — Middle East & Africa (`assets/jurisdiction/national/{ae,za,ng,ke,sa,il,eg}.json`, new):**
- Direct research fork (not the runtime `jurisdiction_search.rs` auto-populate path — that one is unverified here due to no configured search API key; this is real web search performed directly, then shipped as static content, the same proven approach as `global.json`).
- Real content with real citations, following the exact `JurisdictionContentFile{disclaimer, rules}` shape:
  - **UAE (AE)** — 4 rules: PDPL (Federal Decree-Law 45/2021, official source u.ae), Wadeema's Law child protection (Federal Law 3/2016, official text hosted by KHDA), cyberbullying (Federal Law 34/2021), Consumer Protection (Federal Law 15/2020, official text at uaelegislation.gov.ae + elaws.moj.gov.ae).
  - **South Africa (ZA)** — 3 rules: POPIA (Act 4/2013), Children's Act 38/2005 (official text at justice.gov.za), Film and Publications Amendment Act 11/2019 (child sexual abuse material).
  - **Nigeria (NG)** — 2 rules: NDPA 2023 (official regulator ndpc.gov.ng, official PDF at cert.gov.ng), FCCPA 2018 (official regulator fccpc.gov.ng).
  - **Kenya (KE)** — 3 rules: Data Protection Act 24/2019 (official text at odpc.go.ke), Consumer Protection Act 46/2012 (parliament.go.ke), Child Online Protection Industry Guidelines (Communications Authority of Kenya, ca.go.ke).
  - **Saudi Arabia (SA)** — 2 rules: PDPL (SDAIA, official regulator sdaia.gov.sa), Anti-Cyber Crime Law 2007 (official text at mcit.gov.sa) — disclaimer explicitly notes independent reporting found during this same research that the Anti-Cyber Crime Law has, in practice, also been used against minors for peaceful expression; recorded as an honesty note, not resolved into a rule.
  - **Israel (IL)** — 2 rules: Privacy Protection Law 5741-1981 + its minors-specific provisions — official text is Hebrew-only, so both rules cite third-party English translations explicitly and are flagged accordingly (`official_source: false`).
  - **Egypt (EG)** — 1 rule: Personal Data Protection Law 151/2020 — official primary text is Arabic-only (Official Gazette); cites an English translation. **Honestly incomplete**: a real search attempt for Egypt child-protection/consumer-protection statutes did not surface a verifiable primary source in the time available — omitted rather than guessed, and the file's disclaimer says so explicitly.
  - Every rule's `action` is `Log`-only (never Block/Warn/RequireConfirmation) — this content was researched and drafted by an automated process, not reviewed by qualified counsel, matching the same conservative posture as the runtime `jurisdiction_search.rs` mechanism.
- All 7 real `JurisdictionRuleSet` containers created live via `/zsei/query` (ids 1103-1109), correctly parented under the real `JurisdictionRoot` (id 7), `object_store_path` pointing at each new file; files also copied into the running dev instance's actual `zsei_data/jurisdiction/national/` (not just `assets/`, which only reaches a *fresh* instance's data dir at first boot).
- **Independently rediscovered, and confirmed already fixed, a real critical bug**: every ISO-3166-1 alpha-2 region code (`ae`, `za`, `ng`, `ke`, `sa`, `il`, `eg` — and the sibling Europe fork's `ch`/`gb`/`no`/`eu`) is exactly 2 characters, and the default `scan` search strategy's length≥3 keyword filter (a real fix from earlier this session, for a different false-positive bug) silently treats every 2-char keyword as noise on **both** the query and haystack side — confirmed live via a direct `SearchContainersByKeywords` query returning `{"Containers":[]}` for a container that demonstrably exists with the right keyword. This would have made every single national-jurisdiction lookup in the whole system return zero results regardless of real content existing, a total, silent failure of this entire feature (mine and the Europe fork's work both). Found `src/orchestrator/adapters.rs` already carries the fix (a sibling process got there first): `JurisdictionRuleSet` lookups now explicitly request the `"exact"` strategy (no length filter, exact-term equality) instead of the length/stopword-filtered default `"scan"`; confirmed the `strategy` field is genuinely threaded all the way through (`types/zsei.rs`'s `ZSEIQuery::SearchContainersByKeywords`, `zsei/query.rs`'s handler, `search_registry.run(strategy.as_deref(), ...)`), not just present at the call site.
- *Not yet confirmed via a live rebuild+restart at time of writing* — multiple concurrent builds already in flight from sibling work when this was written; deliberately did not start a redundant one under memory pressure. Once any in-progress build lands and the server restarts, a real test would be setting `instance_region` to one of these 7 codes (or checking this dev machine's own auto-detected `"DO"` once real Dominican Republic content exists) and confirming `rules_loaded` in the Jurisdiction Gate stage log reflects real national-scope rules, not just the 7 Global ones.

### Task persistence / checkpointing
- Real bug found and fixed: checkpoint-saving methods (`update_step`, `set_thinking_log`, `set_amt_summary`) existed and looked functional but never called `save_to_disk()` — step progress was memory-only, a crash would have silently lost everything.
- Boot-time reconciliation: any task stuck `"queued"`/`"running"` from an unclean shutdown is now marked `"interrupted"` with its real partial progress intact.
- Task-status double-quoting bug fixed (`format!("{:?}", ...)` on an already-`String` field wrapped every status in literal quote characters).

### Model reliability
- Retry loops (step execution, meta-loop, AMT-loop) now treat an empty/unusable `response` field as a real failure worth retrying/falling back from — not just hard errors. This was the actual mechanism behind repeated silent failures observed all session (OpenRouter returning technically-successful-but-empty completions).
- Real model-fallback escalation on retry, shared logic extracted (`walk_fallback_chain_standalone`) so the two background loops use the exact same real chain-walking the live request path does, not a reimplementation.
- `model_used` now reports the **real model the API actually routed to** (reads the response's own `model` field), not just the requested identifier — previously "openrouter/auto" was echoed back verbatim regardless of what really served the call, making the already-built UI "⇄ switched to X" notification silently dead. *Built and code-reviewed this session; not yet confirmed with a live call (pending — see Testing section).*

### Subsystem audit (network / auth / integrity / consciousness / monitor / pairing / MCP / browser automation)
Same pattern confirmed again — real infrastructure, several places with zero application-level hookup:
- **Auth session validation** — real and working, verified for `/pipeline/execute` (`src/auth/mod.rs:259-276`, genuine token lookup + expiry, real rejection on failure). *Not confirmed whether `/orchestrate` itself is gated the same way — every test this session sent no session_token and worked.*
- **ActivityHub/Monitor** — real and working; `record()` is genuinely called from the real pipeline-execution path, `/monitor/summary` returns real data, not placeholders.
- **Device pairing** (`src/pairing.rs`) — confirmed real, complete, and produces a genuinely usable session: `approve()` validates against live sessions, then calls the real `AuthSystem` to create an actual user/device session (same storage backing normal logins, not a stub) — not just a "looks successful" response.
- **Network P2P sync** — real transport layer (genuine libp2p dial/gossipsub), but `on_methodology_created`/`on_blueprint_created`/`on_container_created` have zero call sites anywhere outside `network/mod.rs` — nothing in `methodology_create`/`blueprint_create`/the orchestrator ever actually triggers a sync. Real transport, disconnected content pipeline.
- **Integrity monitoring** — the loop genuinely runs (hourly, real blake3 hashing, real corruption detection, real rollback-with-reverification logic), but `create_snapshot` — the only way data ever enters what the loop checks — has zero call sites anywhere. A fully-real detection engine with nothing to detect (`containers_checked` always 0).
- **MCP tool registry** — real, working in-memory registry with real HTTP endpoints to register external tools, but no invocation code exists anywhere — you can register that a tool exists; nothing can actually call it yet.
- **`browser_navigation` pipeline (25)** — genuinely real implementation (shells out to real Puppeteer via Node, real Xvfb/x11vnc virtual-display management — architecturally the same pattern as the already-trusted BitNet subprocess approach). **Fixed and now builds clean** (same `E0716` temporary-value-dropped-while-borrowed bug class as code modality earlier tonight — `port.to_string()` bound to a local before referencing). Still unverified for actual function — needs `node`/Puppeteer installed on the host, not confirmed present.

## ⚠️ Partial / Known Limitations

- **12 of 15 bootstrap methodologies** still have zero real content (name/keywords only) — only Clean Code, Code Review, and Security were populated.
- **`methodology_create`'s `Update` action** has a separate, real content-persistence bug: writes a delta-only file, never updates `object_store_path` — found, not fixed (out of scope of the fork that found it).
- **Zero-Shot Simulation inefficiency**: when the model decides clarification is needed, the orchestrator still executes all blueprint steps and spends real tokens *before* discarding those results in favor of showing clarification questions — flagged twice, not fixed.
- **Web search's actual `Search` action**: mechanism is real and honest, but never verified against a real Brave API response (no key available in-session).
- **Voice pipeline**: real, honest implementation, but unconfigured (disabled, no model path set) and never tested live.
- **Automatic resume**: interrupted tasks are cleanly detected and marked, but nothing yet re-enters step execution at the exact interruption point — deliberately deferred as a separate, larger feature.
- **All 6 bootstrap blueprints are empty shells over HTTP** — worse than the methodology version of this bug: real, rich blueprint content (5+ real steps each, real `pipeline_id`s/methodology requirements) already exists at `zsei_data/local/blueprints/*.json`, but `copy_blueprints()` copies from `assets/blueprints/`, which **doesn't exist at all** — so this real content is permanently stranded and never reaches a fresh instance. Compounding: even if the path were fixed, the stranded files use an old schema (`{step_id, name, ...}`) incompatible with the real `BlueprintStep` struct (`step_index`, `context_requirements`, `depends_on`, ...) — a schema migration is needed too, not just a path fix. The 100%-match blueprint-reuse path itself (`stages.rs:171-182`) is real, not a stub — it's just never reachable with usable content today.
- **Category system is one-directional** — real category containers get created/searched for real (driving the "Categories: N created" stage output seen all session), but every methodology/blueprint construction site hardcodes `category_ids`/`categories: []` — nothing ever links a methodology or blueprint back to the category meant to contain it.
- **UI/UX Phase 2 (from the original plan) is the one genuinely incomplete phase**: `.opanel` CSS fill and the real P2P status badge were done, but `StatusBar.tsx`'s `totalContributions`/`methodologiesShared`/`blueprintsShared`/`findingsShared`/`zseiContainers`/`zseiDepth` are still fabricated placeholder values — never actually removed as the plan called for (the component's own comment now honestly documents this instead of hiding it, which is progress, but the fields themselves remain fake).
- **Dead code**: `TaskManager::start_refinement_daemon()` (`src/task/mod.rs`) still exists with zero call sites anywhere — fully superseded by the real, working `meta_loop.rs`/`amt_loop.rs` built this session, but never removed. Cosmetic/confusing, not a functional bug.
- **Critical BitNet fallback bug, found and fixed live tonight**: when a step falls back from a large-context model (e.g. OpenRouter, 128K context) to BitNet (real context ~4-8K), `max_tokens` for generation was carried over unchanged from the original model's budget (e.g. 32000) with zero relation to BitNet's actual context window. At BitNet's real ~7-9 tokens/sec, this meant a genuine 60+ minute generation that looked exactly like a hang — confirmed live (a real request stuck 20+ minutes was actually `llama-cli` correctly grinding through `-n 32000`). Fixed in both `execute_bitnet` and the sibling `execute_gguf` path: generation is now capped to half of the model's own real context window regardless of what the caller originally requested.
- **`/orchestrate` has no authentication at all.** `OrchestrateRequest.session_token` exists as a field but the handler never reads it — every real test this entire session worked with no token sent. Contrast: `/pipeline/execute` genuinely validates sessions and rejects bad ones. No comment anywhere marks this as an intentional local-only trust boundary — reads as a real gap, not a documented design choice. Worth explicit attention if this is ever exposed beyond localhost.
- **Task concurrency limiting is dead config.** `TaskQueueConfig.max_concurrent` (default 5) is only ever read inside `start_queue_processor`, which (like the old refinement daemon) has zero callers anywhere. Every `/orchestrate` request runs as its own unbounded concurrent task — nothing currently throttles simultaneous orchestrations.
- **ZSEI has no real embeddings anywhere, despite the name.** "Zero-Shot **Embedded** Indexer" — `ZSEIQuery::SemanticSearch` has no handler (unreachable), `semantic_traversal` is keyword-overlap despite its name/doc comment, every container construction site sets `embedding: null`, and there is zero embedding-generation or vector-similarity code anywhere in the codebase. Every search mechanism found and tested this entire session (methodology, blueprint, category, text/code keywords) is plain string matching, not vector similarity. This is a foundational architecture gap, not a small one.
- **K-ALGORITHM family status, precisely re-verified** (`src/k_registry.rs`, `src/zsei/search.rs`):
  - `convergence` / `pairwise`: live, real consumer (`orchestrator/amt.rs`), runtime-switchable via `set_convergence_preset`/`set_pairwise_preset` and exposed in `config.toml`'s `[k_algorithms]`.
  - `validation` / `ordered_loop`: genuinely unused — no live consumer anywhere, matches the module's own doc comment, changing their preset has zero observable effect.
  - `search`: **correction to the earlier note** — the underlying strategy logic (`KeywordScan`/`ExactMatch` in `src/zsei/search.rs`) is very much LIVE and real (it's the engine behind `SearchContainersByKeywords`, used throughout context aggregation — this is the exact code with the false-positive bug found and fixed this session, see above). What's actually unused is the *registry facade's own instance* (`KAlgorithms::global().search`): ZSEI's query handler (`src/zsei/query.rs:40`) constructs its own separate, independent `SearchRegistry::new()` rather than reading from the global K-ALGORITHM facade, so `KAlgorithms::global().search`'s `register()`/`set_default()` are dead — they'd have zero effect on the real search path since nothing live ever reads from that particular instance. Currently harmless (both instances start with identical shipped presets/strategies, so behavior is the same either way), but it means "switch search strategy at runtime" isn't wired the way convergence/pairwise already are, despite the registry existing for exactly that. Not fixed this session — flagged as a real, if low-impact, wiring gap.
- **ZCode connector** (`tools/zcode-connector/connect.js`) — real, well-designed scaffolding (correct pipeline-registration, heartbeat/reconnect with backoff, a local server implementing the exact real pipeline-9 wire contract), iterated on twice this session. But `handleModelCall()` — the one function that would actually invoke ZCode — is an intentional stub that throws rather than fabricate a fake completion. The generic remote-pipeline mechanism it depends on (`register_remote_pipeline`/`RemotePipelines::execute`) is real and confirmed working elsewhere; ZCode itself was never actually connected end-to-end.
- **No real logging/metrics/observability system exists anywhere** — "logging" is exclusively human-readable `tracing` log lines (zero `prometheus`/`metrics`/`opentelemetry` dependencies anywhere). `hotness_score`/`access_frequency` (real, present-in-schema container fields) are write-only — set once at creation, never read, aggregated, decayed, or surfaced anywhere. No `/metrics` endpoint, no dashboard, no rollup of anything across requests/time.
- **`docs/` meaningfully lags behind tonight's real work.** Only the jurisdiction gate got real documentation (`docs/JURISDICTION_SETUP.md`, created tonight). Zero mention anywhere in `docs/` of the methodology meta-loop, the AMT re-expansion loop, the web search pipeline, or the `SubprocessExecutor` pipeline-internal-LLM-call architecture — all real, substantial, tested features that exist only in code comments and this checklist.

## Bug found + fixed: primary model defaulted to paid OpenRouter router despite zero credits

Root cause of every OpenRouter failure tonight (not a code bug, a config default): both `config.toml` files' `[models]` block had `api_model = "openrouter/auto"` — OpenRouter's **paid** intelligent auto-router, which bills the account per-call. This account has never purchased credits, so every call 402'd instantly, retried a few times against the same doomed model, then fell through to the BitNet fallback (working correctly, just slow).

The free-tier infrastructure was **already fully built** and just wasn't being used as primary: both configs already had an `openrouter/free` entry in `available_models` (`is_free = true`), and `target/release/config.toml` already had a `[models.meta_fallback]` section with `free_only = true`. This was a wiring/default bug, not a missing feature.

Fixed: changed `api_model` to `"openrouter/free"` and reordered `[models.fallback]` to try `bitnet-i2_s` → `openrouter/free` → `openrouter/auto` (paid, last resort) in both `config.toml` (root) and `target/release/config.toml` (the one actually loaded, since the server's CWD is `target/release`).

**OpenRouter free-tier limits (confirmed via live web search, since this moves fast and is past model knowledge cutoff):**
- 20 requests/minute, always.
- 50 requests/day on an account that has never purchased credits.
- 1,000 requests/day once $10+ in credits has been purchased at any point (lifetime, even if balance later drops back to $0) — buying credits does NOT raise the per-minute cap, only the daily one.
- `openrouter/free` (the identifier used here) is OpenRouter's own real free-only auto-router — launched Feb 2026, randomly selects among currently-available `:free`-suffixed models. Confirmed as a real, currently-live model identifier via OpenRouter's own docs (not guessed).
- Daily reset timing: most sources say UTC midnight; at least one source describes it as a rolling window instead. Not fully confirmed from OpenRouter's own docs — treat as approximate.

- [x] **Confirmed live**: a real request against `openrouter/free` completes end-to-end with no 402, no hang — 237.9s total for a trivial one-line prompt (see new findings below).
- [x] **Confirmed live**: `model_used` fix genuinely works — 7 distinct real model identifiers observed across one request's internal calls (`nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b:free`, `inclusionai/ling-3.0-flash-fin:free`, `cohere/north-mini-code:free`, `poolside/laguna-xs-2.1:free`, `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-content-safety:free`, `inclusionai/ling-3.0-flash-vl:free`, `nex-agi/nex-n2.5-mini:free`), confirming the fix reads each response's own `model` field rather than echoing a static value.

## New findings from the first real openrouter/free live test (2026-09-14)

- **Over-eager clarification-seeking**: a completely unambiguous prompt ("Say hello in exactly one short sentence.") triggered `needs_clarification: true` with 5 clarifying questions (tone? recipient? error-handling branches? language? a verification step?) and `success: false`. A second test with a genuinely ambiguous prompt ("Plan a marketing campaign for our product.") got `needs_clarification: false` structurally (though the model's own generated text asked questions anyway) — confirming this isn't "always triggers," it's inconsistent/model-luck-dependent since free-tier auto-routing picks a different (and differently-calibrated) model per internal call.
  - **Root cause found** (`src/orchestrator/stages.rs`'s `stage_4_zero_shot_simulation`, ~line 706): the simulation prompt asked the model to predict "potential issues or clarifications needed" as one of three required fields per step with zero guidance on the bar for when clarification is actually warranted — any non-empty result unconditionally set `needs_clarification = true` → `success: false`, blocking the whole response.
  - **Fixed**: rewrote the prompt to explicitly require BLOCKING ambiguity only (no reasonable default exists) and explicitly list common non-blocking cases (tone, audience, language, format, error-handling preferences) the model should silently default on instead of asking. **Confirmed live**: both a rebuilt "say hello" retest and a rebuilt SAST-audit retest came back `needs_clarification: false` — verified across multiple retests, no longer model-luck-dependent.

- **CRITICAL bug found: near-universal false-positive container matching, corrupting step context.** The SAST-audit test (`"Write step-by-step guidance for conducting a static application security testing (SAST) audit..."`) took 921.8s and returned complete garbage — a dump of ZSEI's internal modality/category-root tree (`"## ModalityRoot: Modality\nKeywords: modality\n\n## Modality: Text..."` etc.) instead of any real content.
  - **Root cause found**: `KeywordScan::search` (`src/zsei/search.rs:39-72`) — the shared, default search strategy behind `SearchContainersByKeywords`, used by every keyword-based ZSEI container search system-wide — does bidirectional substring matching (`haystack.contains(keyword) || keyword.contains(haystack)`) with **no minimum keyword length**. The query included the single-character word `"a"` (an ordinary word in "...audit on **a** Rust codebase"), and `"a"` is a substring of nearly every keyword in the store (`"modality"`, `"math"`, `"data"`, `"external"`, ...). Every generic structural-root container in the store matched, flooding the step's context with ~25 irrelevant entries that buried the real instruction, and the model (free-tier, and the actual task instruction was positioned LAST after the noise) produced a garbled regurgitation of the context instead of a real answer. 550s of that request's 921.8s total was the single Step Execution call processing this bloated, noise-dominated prompt.
  - **Fixed** (two changes, compounding the same failure mode from different angles):
    1. `src/zsei/search.rs`: `KeywordScan::search` now filters out keywords shorter than 3 characters before matching — they carry effectively no discriminating signal and are the direct cause of the universal-substring problem. Fixes this for every keyword-based container search host-wide, not just this one call site.
    2. `src/orchestrator/stages.rs`'s step-execution prompt template: reordered so "Original request" leads and background context trails, explicitly labeled as optional/ignorable — defense-in-depth so a weak model given legitimately-long context still can't lose track of what it's actually supposed to produce.
  - **Second round found live, after the length-3 fix was already running**: a fresh "Say hello in exactly one short sentence." request still pulled in an entirely unrelated, previously-stored photosynthesis container (`## ModalityGraph: Unknown text graph (29 words...)\nKeywords: photosynthesis, plants, chlorophyll...`) from earlier testing this same session. Root cause: the query keyword `"the"` (an ordinary 3-letter stopword, present in almost any sentence, so it survives a bare length≥3 filter) is itself a substring of **"pho-to-SYNTHE-sis"**. Also found the length filter had only ever been applied to the query side — the haystack (stored container keywords/topics) was never filtered at all, so a short *stored* keyword could symmetrically slip through the same way.
  - **Fixed**: added a real (if non-exhaustive) stopword list (`the`, `and`, `for`, `are`, `this`, `that`, `have`, etc. — ~50 of the highest-frequency English function words) to `src/zsei/search.rs`, filtered on BOTH the query side and the haystack side, alongside the existing length≥3 check.
  - **Also found while investigating**: an existing K-ALGORITHM policy in this codebase is "algorithms are never removed, only superseded — an old implementation gets preserved under its own name as a selectable, non-default option" (not something I introduced — this is how a `/btw` fork correctly resolved a conflict with my first length-3 fix, converting it into `KeywordScan` (new default, id `"scan"`) + `LooseScan` (old pre-fix behavior, preserved and selectable as `"scan-legacy"`) rather than deleting the old behavior outright). The stopword fix applies to the new default (`KeywordScan`) only, per that same policy — `LooseScan` intentionally keeps the original, unfixed behavior available for selection.
  - **K-ALGORITHM search-family wiring note** (see the corrected K-ALGORITHM section below): this `search.rs` logic is the real, live implementation regardless of these fixes — but note `KAlgorithms::global().search`'s own registry instance is disconnected from the real request path (ZSEI's query handler builds its own separate `SearchRegistry::new()`), so switching the default strategy via the K-ALGORITHM facade currently has no live effect; only editing the strategy's own code (as done here) reaches production.
  - **Confirmed live end-to-end after restart**: the same SAST-audit prompt that previously returned a ZSEI category-tree dump now returns a real, correctly-formatted, on-topic answer (`"# Step-by-Step Guidance: Conducting a SAST Audit on a Rust Codebase... ## Phase 1: Tool Selection ### 1.1 Identify Rust-Specific SAST Tools | Tool | Type | Strengths | Best For |..."`) — genuinely relevant content, no leaked calculator/photosynthesis containers, no structural-root dump. Both fixes (this one and the step-prompt reordering) hold up under a real retest.

- **Free-tier latency is real**: 237.9s (~4 min) for a trivial "say hello" prompt even without the above bug. Not a hang — the actual cost of routing every internal call through free-tier OpenRouter models (7 sequential internal LLM calls, 5,685 tokens total for that one request). Worth knowing before mistaking a slow-but-working free-tier response for a stuck one.

- **New CRITICAL bug found: BitNet degenerate token repetition, no anti-repetition sampling configured.** A retest of "Say hello in exactly one short sentence." (after the clarification-overreach fix and search-relevance fix — `needs_clarification` correctly came back `false` this time, confirming that fix works) took 2301s (38+ min) and still returned garbage: `"## Pipeline: Core\nKeywords: core, core, core, core, ..."` repeated dozens of times. Step Execution alone took 1,364,277ms (~22.7 min) and burned ~10,008 tokens — almost entirely the word "core" repeated.
  - **Root cause found**: `execute_bitnet`/`execute_gguf` (`assets/pipelines/general/prompt/main.rs`) invoke `llama-cli` with zero repetition-control flags (no `--repeat-penalty`, no `--repeat-last-n`) — nothing stops this small, heavily quantized (1-bit) local model from looping on a single token once it starts, burning its entire generation budget and wall-clock time on garbage instead of stopping or producing real content.
  - **Fixed**: added `--repeat-penalty 1.1 --repeat-last-n 256` (standard llama.cpp defaults) to both functions' `llama-cli` invocations.
  - Also worth noting: this test ran under heavy CPU contention (4-5 concurrent `llama-cli` processes at once — 2 manual test retests + the methodology meta-loop drafting 2 real gaps back-to-back), which likely made the degenerate repetition worse/slower to hit its token cap, but contention is NOT the root cause of the repetition itself — that's the missing sampling parameter, confirmed by the complete absence of any repeat-penalty flag in the command.
  - **Confirmed live after restart**: retested "say hello" — routed through OpenRouter free this time rather than BitNet (so this specific fix wasn't exercised by that run), but no repetition garbage appeared in either retest. Not yet caught BitNet in the act of a real generation post-fix to directly confirm the repeat-penalty flags themselves stop a degenerate loop — the underlying `llama-cli` flags are confirmed present and correct in the rebuilt binary, but a live BitNet-routed retest is still worth catching opportunistically (e.g. next time free-tier quota is exhausted) rather than manufactured, since forcing it would mean deliberately breaking the API config.

### Pattern investigated: "Blueprint Assignment" stage consistently ~580s — resolved, not a bug
Across two clean retests (hello: 581.7s, SAST: 580.0s — nearly identical despite completely different prompts), the Blueprint Assignment stage alone took ~9.7 minutes both times. Traced `walk_fallback_chain_standalone` (`src/orchestrator/mod.rs:2231`): it tries each fallback candidate exactly once in order, no hidden retry loop — ruled out a retry-storm bug. Most likely real explanation: this session's heavy concurrent LLM load (multiple manual test retests plus the methodology meta-loop firing at the same time) intermittently hits OpenRouter free's hard 20-req/min cap, causing that specific stage's primary call to fail and fall through to the fallback order's first candidate, `bitnet-i2_s` — whose real cost (model load + up to 1000-token generation at BitNet's genuine ~7-9 tok/s) is roughly constant regardless of prompt content, which is exactly why two unrelated prompts landed on nearly the same duration. Consistent with the "hello" retest's own stage timings, where Blueprint Assignment was slow (bitnet fallback) but the later Step Execution stage in the *same request* was fast (2s, real OpenRouter free success) — the rate-limit window had likely cleared by then. Not a code defect; just the already-built fallback system doing its job under real infrastructure constraints (rate limit + BitNet's genuine slowness). No fix needed here beyond what's already landed (repeat-penalty, max_tokens cap).

Bangladesh has **no express online/digital-specific child-safety law** — the child-protection rule cites the real general Children Act 2013 and explicitly notes the absence of any online-specific provision (no mandated age verification, no CSAM takedown obligation) rather than implying one exists.

## Math modality (pipeline 105, assets/pipelines/modalities/math) — built and working for the first time ever

Same starting state code modality was in before this session: 2316 lines of real, substantial pre-existing source (rich proof/expression data model — MathGraph, ProofAnalysis, ScopeTree, etc.) that had never actually been built or exercised end-to-end. Brought up to real parity with text/code modality, plus one gap (real graph retrieval) that neither of those two actually have working yet either.

**Bugs found and fixed:**
- **CLI contract**: `main()` read `args[1]` positionally — the host invokes every pipeline with `--input <json> --execution-id ...`, so any real invocation got the literal string `"--input"` as its "JSON" and failed instantly. Replaced with the standard `parse_cli_input()` pattern (matching code/text modality): `--input` flag, `{data, context}` envelope unwrap, bare-positional-arg fallback kept for manual testing.
- **Zero real ZSEI persistence**: `create_graph` built a real in-memory graph then discarded it (`graph_id` was a local nanosecond timestamp, backed by nothing); `get_graph` was an explicit hardcoded stub (`// In production, load from ZSEI`) that always returned an empty graph regardless of `graph_id`. Added real `zsei_query`/`persist_graph_container` (mirrors code modality's pattern exactly — real `CreateContainer` call, real on-disk `graphs/math_{id}.json`, `parent_id = project_id` convention) and wired `create_graph` to call it, replacing the local id with the real container id on success (graceful fallback to local-only on persistence failure, not a hard failure).
- **Real retrieval — a gap neither text nor code modality actually has fixed either**: both of those route `GetGraph` through an in-process `HashMap` cache that's empty on every invocation (each CLI call is a fresh, short-lived process) — confirmed by reading their current source. Implemented real retrieval for math instead of mirroring the same broken pattern: `read_graph_container` queries `GetContainer` for the real `object_store_path` and reads that file back. **Live-verified**: created a graph in one process, retrieved it correctly (matching name/nodes/edges/timestamps) from a completely separate process invocation given only the container id. Worth applying the same fix to text/code's `GetGraph` — flagged, not in scope here.
- **`keywords`/`topics` were never populated on constructed containers** (same bug class that broke methodology/context search everywhere else this session) — added `derive_math_keywords`: real structural extraction (no LLM needed, same reasoning as code modality) from variable names, proof technique, analysis type, and referenced axioms/theorems/definitions/domain.
- **`link_to_modality` and `trigger_semantic_hook` were 100% fabricated stubs** — `trigger_semantic_hook` literally hardcoded `nodes_processed: 15, edges_added: 8, annotations_added: 20` regardless of input, every time, with zero reference to the actual graph. Fixed: `trigger_semantic_hook` now reports real counts from the actual persisted graph (`annotations_added` stays honestly `0` — no real semantic-enrichment logic exists yet, and reporting a fabricated non-zero number implying it does would be worse than reporting an honest zero). `link_to_modality` now does a real, bidirectional ZSEI relationship (`GetContainer` → append to `context.relationships` → `UpdateContainer`, on both containers) instead of fabricating a `LinkResult` for graph ids that might not even exist.
- **Real bug found along the way**: the shared `Modality` enum (`src/types/container.rs`) has no `Math` variant at all (only `Unknown/Text/Code/Image/Audio/Video/Graph/TimeSeries/Structured/External/Multimodal`) — it predates math/chemistry/dna/etc as distinct pipeline modalities. Used `Structured` as the most honest real fit (formal symbolic content, not prose/code/media) rather than fabricating a category; documented clearly in source. Affects every future non-text/code/media modality equally — worth a real enum extension later, not fixed here (would touch shared host code, out of this task's scope).
- A real, pre-existing (not introduced by this work) `E0382` partial-move compile error in `query_graph`'s `GetStepsByType` arm, parallel to one the main session fixed in `GetNodesByType` — fixed the same way (`ref step_type`, adjusted the `discriminant()` call site for the now-reference type).

**Live end-to-end verification** (ran the built binary directly against the real running dev server's ZSEI, via the same `/zsei/query` HTTP path the host itself uses):
1. `ParseExpression` on `x^2 + 2*x + 1 = 0` — real parse, real variable/operation detection.
2. `CreateGraph` from a real proof analysis — real container created (id 1007), real node/edge graph, real keywords derived.
3. **`GetGraph` in a separate process invocation, given only the container id** — correctly retrieved the exact same content (name, node count, timestamps) created by the prior process. This is the core "does persistence actually survive across the real one-shot-subprocess-per-call architecture" question, and it does.
4. **Methodology/container relationship — the user's explicit ask**: `curl /zsei/query` `SearchContainersByKeywords` with real math keywords (`"direct"`, `"square"`) correctly found container 1007 and only that container (no false positives) — confirming math content is discoverable via the exact same, already-fixed `KeywordScan` search mechanism (`src/zsei/search.rs`, stopword + length≥3 fix) that methodology search and every other keyword-based ZSEI lookup uses. No special-case wiring was needed — populating real keywords/topics was sufficient.
5. `LinkToModality` between two real containers (1006, 1007) — confirmed via direct `GetContainer` reads afterward that both containers now carry a real, bidirectional `Relation` entry pointing at each other.
6. `TriggerSemanticHook` on container 1007 — returned `nodes_processed: 3, edges_added: 2`, matching that container's actual real graph exactly (no longer the hardcoded 15/8/20).

**Shared-pattern generalization — assessed, not extracted this pass**: `parse_cli_input`'s CLI-contract logic and the `zsei_query`/`ozone_host` pair are now byte-for-byte identical across text, code, and math modality — genuine, real duplication worth extracting into a small shared crate (alongside the existing `shared/contracts` used by K-ALGORITHM) that all modality pipelines could depend on. Container-construction JSON shares its outer shape (global_state/local_state/metadata/context/storage/hints/integrity) but per-modality keyword/name logic differs meaningfully, so only the outer builder is a clean extraction candidate, not the whole thing. Deliberately NOT done in this pass: doing so now means touching text and code modality's already-working, already-tested source purely for a refactor, which is separate scope from "get math modality working" and carries real regression risk for no functional benefit today — a real, appropriately-scoped follow-up task, not force-fit here.

## Web search bridge for ZCode (and any future non-Claude-Code agent) — built, real round-trip verified

Context: pipeline 56 (`assets/pipelines/general/web_search`) needs a real `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` to work and this host has none — confirmed, no key found anywhere in the environment (`env | grep -i brave`, shell profiles, config.toml). The user explicitly chose to route search needs through Claude Code's own real, working native search tool instead of provisioning a Brave key. Pipeline 56 itself was left untouched, per the user's own earlier-negotiated scope — this is a separate, additive coordination path.

**What was built**, `tools/ozone-shared-context/server.js` (now `server_version: "0.3.0"`, bumped from 0.2.0 since a real capability was added):
- Four new MCP tools: `search_request` (any agent posts a query, gets an id back immediately — non-blocking, no guaranteed latency), `search_pending` (list unanswered requests — what a live Claude Code session checks), `search_result` (poll by id — `status: "pending"` or `"answered"` with real results), `answer_search` (fulfill a pending request with results actually retrieved via a real search tool — the tool description explicitly warns against fabricating results to close a request faster).
- New state file `.ozone-context/search_requests.jsonl` — one record per request, overwritten in place on answer (not append-a-second-record) so polling by id is a single lookup.
- `node --check tools/ozone-shared-context/server.js` — syntax-clean.

**Real round-trip test performed** (the MCP tool itself isn't reachable from this Claude Code session either — same harness gap noted below — so tested via a standalone script replicating the file-I/O logic exactly, mirroring how the main session verified `.ozone-context/` writes all along): posted a real request ("what is the current version of Rust as of September 2026"), performed an actual web search with Claude Code's real search tool, wrote back real results (Rust 1.98.1, real URLs to blog.rust-lang.org and releases.rs), polled by id and got the real answer back correctly. Test artifacts removed from `.ozone-context/search_requests.jsonl` afterward — it's canonical shared state, not a scratch file.

**Open question, genuinely unresolved**: whether ZCode's own runtime can reach these MCP tools at all — the main session independently confirmed twice tonight (long-running session, and a fresh fork launch where the "approve on first use" trigger should fire) that Claude Code cannot reach `ozone-shared-context`'s tools in this environment; a harness-level gap, not a bug in this server. Whether ZCode's own MCP client implementation has the same limitation is unknown from here — not assumed either way.

**How to use this right now, without waiting on that gap to resolve** (the part that's actually usable today, proven by the round-trip test above): any agent — including a future Claude Code session working the same direct-file-access way the main session has been operating all along — can read/write `.ozone-context/search_requests.jsonl` directly following the exact shape `server.js`'s functions use (documented in the file's own comments): append `{id, requester, query, status:"pending", at}` to request, find-and-overwrite the matching record with `status:"answered", results, answered_by, answered_at` to fulfill it.

## Task 42/46 closed — ZSEI coordination mirroring + methodology 16 registration, both independently verified

- **ZCode's task 42 (ZSEI container mirroring of coordination state)**: real, confirmed independently via `GetContainer(8)` — the new `/SharedContext` root genuinely has 2 real child containers (ids 30104, 30105), matching exactly what was predicted before checking. `server.js` v0.3.1 now auto-mirrors `note_add`/`file_claim` calls into real ZSEI containers, not just the `.ozone-context/` files.
- **My task 46 (verify methodology 16 registers as a ZSEI container)**: confirmed independently via `GetContainer(METHODOLOGY_ROOT_ID=2)` — genuinely shows `child_count: 16`, meaning Methodology 16 (Host Lifecycle Management) is a real, live, queryable container, not just an index-file entry. Task closed.
- **Real bug found and fixed**: `src/orchestrator/meta_loop.rs:168` and the identical pattern in `src/orchestrator/amt_loop.rs:226` computed fallback-model escalation as `((attempts - 1)).min(fallback_candidates.len() - 1)` — this clamps to the last fallback candidate forever once the list is exhausted, instead of cycling through all of them. With `meta_fallback.order = [bitnet-i2_s, openrouter/free]`, every retry attempt from #4 onward got stuck retrying the same `openrouter/free` (broken tonight — daily quota exhausted) forever, never giving `bitnet-i2_s` (a real, working fallback, just slower) another turn. Confirmed live: one real gap ("communication/conciseness/greeting") reached attempt 9 of the 10-attempt cap with 6 of those attempts wasted on the same guaranteed-fail target. Fixed: `.min(len - 1)` → `% len` in both files, so every retry now cycles through the full fallback list instead of getting stuck. This answers the user's direct question ("is the meta-loop creating methodologies correctly?") — the drafting/creation logic itself was always correct; this retry-escalation bug was the real obstacle under tonight's specific failure conditions.
- **Real, ongoing architecture gap found** (not yet fixed, flagged for a future task): `/orchestrate` ties server-side orchestration work to the requesting client's HTTP connection lifetime. When a local curl client got killed by memory pressure (unrelated to the server, which stayed healthy), the already-spawned BitNet subprocess kept computing for a genuine 23 minutes afterward, then finished — but its result was never captured (task record showed `progress:0, steps:[]` even after completion), most likely because the client disconnect cancelled the async request-handler future that would have consumed the subprocess's output. Real wasted compute, worth fixing eventually so long-running orchestrate work survives a dropped client connection.

## Jurisdiction content deepened — 5 countries, 9 new real rules on AI regulation + digital accessibility

Additive pass over the existing 26-country + EU jurisdiction content, adding real, search-verified rules on topics beyond the original 3 (minors protection, data privacy, consumer protection). All existing rules untouched, `action: Log` throughout, matching the established pattern exactly.

- **EU** (+3 rules): EU AI Act (Regulation (EU) 2024/1689) — general provisions/prohibitions (in force since 2 Feb 2025), general-purpose AI model obligations (in force since 2 Aug 2025), Article 50 transparency obligations (in force from 2 Aug 2026). Also explicitly noted which AI Act obligations are NOT yet in force (high-risk system rules, deferred to Dec 2027/Aug 2028) so this isn't mistaken for a current obligation.
- **US** (+2 rules): digital accessibility — Section 508 (federal agencies + government ICT vendors, WCAG 2.0 AA) and ADA Title II's 2024 DOJ rule (state/local government websites, WCAG 2.1 AA, April 2026 deadline). Explicitly noted these cover government entities specifically, not general private-sector services. Deliberately did NOT add a federal AI-regulation rule — no comprehensive binding federal AI law exists to cite; honest omission, not a gap to fill later with something fabricated.
- **China** (+2 rules): Interim Measures for the Management of Generative AI Services (binding since 15 Aug 2023, applies to any service reaching Chinese users regardless of origin) and the newer Interim Measures for AI Anthropomorphic Interactive Services (effective 15 Jul 2026).
- **South Korea** (+1 rule): AI Basic Act — the world's first comprehensive national AI framework after the EU's, in force since 22 Jan 2026, with a grace period on enforcement through at least 2026.
- **UK** (+1 rule): Public Sector Bodies (Websites and Mobile Applications) Accessibility Regulations 2018 — WCAG 2.2 AA for public sector sites/apps, in force since 2018/2021. Noted this covers public sector bodies specifically.

All 5 files validated as parseable JSON, mirrored into `target/release/zsei_data/jurisdiction/national/` (the live server's actual data directory) — since `load_jurisdiction_rules` reads these files fresh per-request rather than caching content in the ZSEI container, these new rules take effect on the next real request with no restart or re-registration needed.

**Not reached this pass** (honest, not silently skipped): the other 21 countries (AE, AR, AU, BR, CA, CH, CL, CO, DO, EG, IL, IN, JP, MX, NG, NO, NZ, SA, SG, ZA) were not deepened — prioritized doing 5 thoroughly over spreading thin across all 27. Also did not research the accessibility/content-moderation topics for EU, China, or South Korea, or the AI-regulation topic for UK — only added what real, current, verifiable content was actually found for each country/topic combination attempted.

## Task 56 (architecture): real fix shipped, deeper traversal gap found and honestly scoped

- **Fixed, built clean (exit 0)**: root-caused tonight's "hallucinated Pipeline catalog" garbage-output bug precisely — NOT the jurisdiction gate (which was working correctly; `matched=0` for a DO-region instance querying EU content is correct behavior). The actual cause: `context_aggregation`'s `ForStep` handler called `search_containers_by_keywords(&keyword_list, None)` — `container_type: None` means a completely type-blind search across the whole ZSEI store, sweeping in Pipeline registry self-description containers (infrastructure metadata, never real content) whenever a query happened to share any keyword with a pipeline's own generic name ("environment", "data", etc.). Fixed with a real container-type exclusion filter (`is_infrastructure_container_type`) in `build_context_from_containers` — excludes Root/User/Workspace/Project/Pipeline/*Root structural types from ever being surfaced as step background context, regardless of which specific keyword caused a match.
- **Found, honestly not yet fixed**: even after wiring real `TraversalEngine` calls into context assembly (the deeper "graph, don't flat-dump" architecture ask), none of its 6 modes (Structural/Semantic/Contextual/Hybrid/MLGuided/BruteForce — confirmed by reading each implementation) actually follow the `Relation`/`Context.relationships` field. A real "jurisdiction meta-workspace" with genuine relationships (e.g. Sweden's national law relates to the EU baseline) needs the traversal engine itself extended to follow relationship edges — not just relationship data added to containers, which nothing would then read. `TraversalEngine` also still has zero live callers even after today's fix (the fix was a targeted filter on the existing flat search, not a rewire to traversal) — that rewire remains real, outstanding work, not claimed complete here.

## Task 56 follow-through (fork): relationship-edge traversal + real jurisdiction graph wired

Direct continuation of the gap the previous entry left honestly open — dispatched to a fork with the explicit directive "the graph wiring finds for all left to do."

- **`src/zsei/traversal.rs` — `structural_traversal` now follows `Relation` edges, not just parent/child.** Confirmed live this session that all 6 traversal modes read `storage.get_children()` only; `Context.relationships` (the real `Relation{target_id, relation_type, confidence}` edges every container already carries) was dead data no traversal mode ever walked. Fixed: after enqueueing structural children, `structural_traversal` now also loads the current container and enqueues every `relationships[].target_id`, with the same `visited`/`max_depth`/path bookkeeping as structural children — so a container's real cross-references are traversable exactly like its tree position. `hybrid_traversal` and `ml_guided_traversal` (falls back to hybrid) inherit this automatically since both call `structural_traversal` directly — confirmed by reading both call sites, no separate edit needed there.
- **`src/lib.rs` — real jurisdiction relationship graph, not just a flat list.** New idempotent, every-boot block (mirrors the existing jurisdiction-container self-heal pattern right above it): every non-"global" `JurisdictionRuleSet` container gets a `RelatedTo` edge to "global" (confidence 0.9), and any EU member state (real ISO-3166-1 alpha-2 codes, all 27, not just the 4 currently populated — so this self-heals for content added later with no further code change) additionally gets a `RelatedTo` edge to "eu". Written via `ZSEIQuery::UpdateContainer` on each container's `context`, skipping any relation that already exists so re-running on every boot never duplicates edges. This is what makes jurisdiction an actual graphed meta-workspace (Sweden → EU → Global, traversable) instead of only being connected by shared keywords.
- **`src/orchestrator/jurisdiction.rs` — `load_jurisdiction_rules` now does real traversal, not just a fixed hand-computed scope list.** Previously it manually built `["global", region, national-prefix-of-region]` and did one flat type-scoped keyword search. Now: the same exact-match keyword lookup still locates each scope's own seed container (this part stays a targeted, type-scoped lookup — not the type-blind scan that caused the earlier hallucination bug), but from each seed it now runs a real `TraversalMode::Structural` traversal (via `StoreAccess::traverse`, already wired end-to-end to `ZSEI::traverse` — confirmed real, not a stub, by reading `ZseiStoreAdapter`) filtered to `container_type == JurisdictionRuleSet`. Because `structural_traversal` now follows `Relation` edges (see above), a national ruleset's traversal genuinely reaches its EU baseline and, from there, Global — without that chain needing to be hand-listed in this function. Global is still always resolved directly as well, since it's a documented hard invariant independent of what the graph happens to connect.
- **Built clean (release, exit 0)** — verified via full `cargo build --release` covering all three files together after all edits landed (an earlier build kicked off mid-edit was intentionally treated as stale and re-run, since it may have read `lib.rs`/`jurisdiction.rs` before the later edits landed).
- **Honestly still open, not claimed done here**: `keyword_filter`/`topic_filter` on `TraversalRequest` remain dead fields (never read anywhere in `traversal.rs`) — not touched by this pass. `ForStep`'s GENERAL context assembly in `context_aggregation/main.rs` still uses the flat type-filtered keyword search (now with the infrastructure-type exclusion fix), not a real `TraversalEngine` call — wiring that through is explicitly separate, larger follow-up work, not rushed into this pass. Jurisdiction relation edges only cover the "global"/"eu" layering described here — no graph edges yet for any other regional bloc (e.g. no ASEAN/AU/Mercosur baseline containers exist to relate to).

## UI/UX modernization (fork): live scroll bug fixed + full frontend audit — most of an existing plan already done, contrary to its own text

Dispatched after the user hit a real, live bug in the running Electron app: during setup, they could not scroll to reach the API key input — "fixed to the size, not dynamic to the app div." Scope: `ui/` only (Vite/React/Electron), no `src/` (Rust backend) changes.

### Fixed and verified live
- **Root cause, `ui/src/App.css`**: `.setup-wizard` (the outer flex container, rendered standalone — `App.tsx` returns `<SetupWizard/>` directly, bypassing the normal `.app{height:100vh;overflow:hidden}` shell) used `min-height:100vh` with no scroll container of its own, while `body{overflow:hidden}` is a real, intentional, app-wide rule (every other panel manages its own internal scroll the same way — confirmed in `.app`/`.app-content`). Net effect: any wizard step taller than the viewport (the model-config step — provider picker, endpoint field, API key field, hints — is exactly that step) was not just hard to scroll, it was **genuinely unreachable**: nothing on the page could scroll to it. Fixed by giving `.setup-wizard` a fixed `height:100vh` + its own `overflow-y:auto` (matching the same bounded-shell pattern `.app` already uses elsewhere in this codebase) and switching `.setup-container` from `align-items:center` to `align-items:flex-start` + `margin:auto 0` — this keeps the visual vertical centering when content fits, but (unlike `align-items:center` alone, which has a well-known cross-browser bug where overflowing centered flex content can only be scrolled from one side) auto-margins collapse to 0 once there's no slack, so every pixel of a tall step stays reachable from the top.
- **Real duplicate CSS found and removed as part of the same fix**: `App.css` had two complete, separate definitions of `.setup-wizard`/`.setup-container`/`.setup-header`/`.setup-progress`/`.setup-content`/`.setup-step`/`.setup-buttons`/`.setup-back`/`.setup-next`/`.setup-complete`/`.setup-summary` — an older block (originally ~line 1875) and a newer, more complete one (originally ~line 3185, the one the current component's classnames — `model-config-section`, `voice-config-section`, `step-number`, etc. — actually depend on). Confirmed via `grep` cross-reference against every classname `SetupWizard.tsx` actually renders that the old block was 100% dead except for two rules (`.connecting-countdown`/`.countdown-number`, still used by `App.tsx`'s separate "connecting" screen) — deleted the dead ~168 lines, kept those two, kept and fixed the live block.
- **Verified live, not just "the CSS looks right now"**: `window.ozone` (the Electron preload bridge `SetupWizard`'s trigger logic depends on) doesn't exist under plain `vite dev`, so a real end-to-end run needed either the packaged Electron app (already running as the user's live session — did not want to disturb it or double up on Electron/Chromium processes on this machine's genuinely tight 7.6GB memory, already under heavy pressure from a concurrent backend LTO build) or a faithful isolated render. Built a minimal HTML harness reproducing the exact live `.setup-wizard > .setup-container > .setup-content > .setup-step` structure with realistic model-config-step content, linked directly against the real (edited) `App.css` — not a transcription — and rendered it with real headless Chromium at two sizes: 400×500 (short) and 320×700 (narrow). Confirmed via actual screenshots: a real scrollbar appears, the API key field is scrollable-to, and a script-driven `scrollIntoView()` + `.focus()` + typed value on the API key input rendered correctly focused and filled mid-scroll — genuinely reachable and typeable, not just theoretically laid out correctly. Screenshots were taken to a scratch location outside the repo and cleaned up after (not committed).

- **Phase 2.1** (`.opanel` not filling space): already fixed — `.opanel` has `display:flex;flex-direction:column;height:calc(100% - 24px)` with a real doc comment describing exactly the fix the plan proposed, plus a dedicated `.opanel-scroll` sub-region class. `.ofeed` has no `max-height` cap. These need `src/` changes; left as-is per this fork's explicit scope boundary (backend work is owned elsewhere in this session right now).
- **Recommendation, not done here**: the plan file itself should be updated or retired/archived rather than left claiming Phases 2-5 are open work — as written it would mislead whoever picks it up next into re-doing already-shipped work.

### New, real finding from this pass: `App.css` has systemic duplicate-selector debt, same bug class as the wizard, much larger in scope
A project-wide scan (top-level selector extraction + count) found roughly 90 distinct selector names defined more than once in `App.css` (6117 lines total) — e.g. `.settings-section`/`.settings-sections` (4 definitions each), `.tasks-list` (4), `.task-info`/`.task-progress` (4), `.meta-portion`/`.meta-prompt` (3), `.filter-btn` (3), `.toggle-switch`/`.toggle-slider` (3), and dozens more with 2 definitions. This is very likely the same root cause as the wizard bug: incremental "vX.Y Updates" comment blocks (confirmed one real example: `/* Setup Wizard v0.4.0 Updates */`) layered on top of the file over time without removing the superseded originals. **Not fixed in this pass** — a naive grep-based duplicate check can't reliably tell a real dead-duplicate (like the wizard's) apart from a legitimate `@media`-scoped responsive override without properly tracking brace nesting, and with ~90 groups across a 6000+ line file, safely resolving each one needs the same per-case "confirm which is live, delete the other, verify by rendering" rigor applied to the wizard above — a real, sizeable follow-up task, honestly scoped rather than rushed or guessed at here. Recommend either a proper CSS-aware pass (a small script that tracks `{`/`}` depth and `@media` context, not string-grep) or introducing `stylelint` with `no-duplicate-selectors` to make this mechanically checkable going forward instead of relying on manual discovery (as both this bug and the wizard bug were found).

### Checklist for next pass (open items, not done here)
- [ ] Resolve the ~90 duplicate-selector groups in `App.css` (see above) — needs proper CSS-depth-aware tooling, not grep.
- [ ] Retire or refresh `~/.claude/plans/inherited-sparking-nest.md` — Phases 2-5 (and part of 6) are done; only Phase 6's backend-coupled remainder (pipeline_id whole-agent routing, per-step `model_override`, `available_models` add/remove ops) is real remaining work, and it needs `src/` changes.
- [ ] `StatusBar.tsx`'s collective-contribution fields (`totalContributions` etc.) are honest placeholders now, but the feature they represent doesn't exist on the backend at all — either build the real stats system or consider removing the UI surface for it entirely rather than showing a permanent "0".
- [ ] Not audited this pass (time-boxed, flagging honestly rather than silently skipping): Pairing/Devices QR flow, ConnectedAgents header widget, Library/Workspace/Tasks panel content beyond the layout-fill fix already confirmed done, accessibility (keyboard nav/focus order/aria labels — none checked), and light-theme support (the app appears to be dark-only by design — no `prefers-color-scheme:light` handling found — worth confirming this is an intentional choice, not an oversight).

Files touched: `ui/src/App.css` (setup-wizard scroll fix + dead-block removal, net ~-137 lines). No `src/` changes, no other `ui/` files changed — everything else audited was confirmed already correct in current source, not modified.

## Task 56 — independently verified end-to-end, real compile error found and fixed in the graph-wiring fork's work

Did not take the prior entry's "Built clean (release, exit 0)" claim on trust — read every diff (`traversal.rs`, `lib.rs`, `jurisdiction.rs`, `bootstrap.rs`) line-by-line against current struct/trait definitions first (all types checked out real), then ran a full `cargo build --release` myself.

- **Real error found**: 2x `E0063 missing field 'discovered_via' in initializer of Relation` at `src/lib.rs:573` and `:587`. The `Relation` struct (`src/types/container.rs:248`) carries a `discovered_via: DiscoveryMethod` field that the fork's two new jurisdiction relationship-edge literals didn't set. Most likely a genuine race — the fork's own build succeeded before `discovered_via` existed on the struct, added by other concurrent work afterward, not fork negligence. Real confirmation that independent re-verification matters even when a self-report says clean, since underlying state can shift between when a fork tests and when its work is reviewed.
- **Fixed**: added `discovered_via: crate::types::container::DiscoveryMethod::Manual` to both `Relation` literals — `Manual` because these are deterministic, hardcoded-rule-asserted edges written at boot (the EU-member-code list + global-baseline rule), not traversal-discovered or ML-predicted.
- **Re-verified clean**: full `cargo build --release` from a cold, contention-checked state (waited out a concurrent in-flight build first rather than compete for this machine's tight memory) — real exit code `0` (captured directly, not via `| tail`), zero `^error` lines, only pre-existing warnings, binary mtime confirmed newer than every touched source file.
- Task 56's full chain (type-exclusion fix → relationship-edge traversal → jurisdiction relationship-graph self-heal → traversal-based `load_jurisdiction_rules`) now genuinely compiles clean end to end, independently confirmed, not just reported. **Not yet live-tested post-restart** — needs a restart to run on the new binary and let the jurisdiction relationship-edge self-heal block execute once; that live verification (confirming a national ruleset's traversal actually reaches its EU baseline via a real `Relation` edge) is still open.
