## 2026-09-29 (zc) — Modality revival batch COMPLETE: chemistry, DNA, EEG all CreateGraph-proven end-to-end and persisted through a live host restart

**The mechanical revival script is now proven, and it worked on all three**: fix the CLI contract (`--input`/stdin + `{data,context}` unwrap), add the `zsei_query` HTTP helper + `persist_*_graph`, wire the `CreateGraph` arm to persist, build, then smoke-test with a fully-materialized input JSON.

**Real results, all via the real binaries against the live host**:
- **chemistry → graph 40520**: water molecule (3 atoms, 2 bonds), 5 nodes / 6 edges. Container: `ModalityGraph`, provenance `chemistry-pipeline`, `graphs/chemistry_40520.json`, keywords h1/h2/o0/water — **retrieved successfully AFTER the operator's live host restart, so the persistence is restart-proven, not just write-and-hope**.
- **DNA → graphs 40525 + 40526**: bare sequence (1 node, proving ORFs correctly earn NO node by design — node types are Sequence/Gene/Exon/Feature/Variant only), then with a gene added: 3 nodes (Sequence + Gene SMK1 + Exon 1) / 2 edges, `provenance: dna-pipeline`. Both containers verified live.
- **EEG → graph 40558**: 3 channels (Fp1/Fp2/Cz) + 2 events (SleepSpindle, KComplex) + 2 band powers → 6 nodes / 5 edges on the FIRST complete input, `provenance: eeg-pipeline`. Container verified live.

**The one real gotcha of the batch, worth remembering for the next modality**: serde input structs have NO `#[serde(default)]` on analysis payloads, so the smoke JSON must carry EVERY field of EVERY nested struct (`Atom.atomic_number`, `Bond.bond_type` as the enum's plain string form `"Single"`, `FrequencyBand` as a struct `{name,low_freq,high_freq}`, unit-only enums as bare strings). Iterating "missing field X → add X" hit 5 rounds on chemistry before a recursive struct-materialization builder (extract all `pub struct` field lists from main.rs, build depth-first, override leaves) produced a complete payload — after that, DNA and EEG went through in 1-2 attempts each. Also: non-Option enum fields given JSON `null` fail with "invalid type: null, expected string or map" — empty the Vec instead of filling it with a null-enum dummy element.

Modalities now live end-to-end: text, math, image, video, audio, 3D, code, **chemistry, DNA, EEG** — 10 of the fleet, each with a real persisted ModalityGraph container and real provenance. Remaining revivals (next batch, same script): the rest of the capability-gap scan list.

4. `spawn_ripple_actor_dispatch` — a new boot-spawned subscriber, same shape as `amt_loop::spawn_graph_ripple_sync`, wired into `src/lib.rs` right after it.
5. **Global (project-less) candidate handling, resolved**: `resolve_or_create_global_amt` — a lazily-created, real, persistent "Global Findings AMT" container under `SharedContext`, explicitly name-scanned (not the generic first-Derived-child heuristic, which would wrongly grab an RE-MCP `RETarget` container) so a finding with no real project anchor still deepens something real and discoverable instead of being silently dropped.
6. **The I-Loop, actually implemented** (`src/orchestrator/i_loop.rs`, new) — real gap closed: pipeline_id 44 was metadata-only since early in the project. Took the guide's Option 1 (long, safe interval — 1800s floor, clamped — mirroring `amt_loop`/`meta_loop`'s own proven convention) over Option 2 (event-driven with undecided trigger signals), and reused the existing generic prompt pipeline (id 9) rather than standing up a whole separate compiled pipeline crate for the "i_loop" folder — smaller, safer, same real outcome. Grounds each reflection in real unhandled-candidate signals, persists as a real, discoverable `CoordinationEvent` (`kind:"i_loop_reflection"`) — never a private log file.

**Refactored `amt_loop.rs` safely** to expose 3 small, reusable pieces the new dispatch loop needs (`notify_wake`, `extract_proj_id`, `resolve_anchor_amt`) — extracted from `process_graph_event`'s own inline logic, behavior-preserving (all 10 pre-existing `amt_loop` tests still pass unchanged).

**3 real bugs found via LIVE end-to-end proof on the actual running host — not just unit tests, which would have missed all three**:
1. **Content-pointer nesting**: `SecurityFindingActor` read `{tool, success}` at the top level of the mirrored tool-call file, but the real file (confirmed live) nests them one level deeper under `detail: {...}` (`context_mirror::mirror`'s real `MirrorRequest` shape). First live test produced zero candidates — silently correct-looking code, wrong shape. Fixed; all synthetic test fixtures corrected to match.
2. **A real feedback loop**: `TaskFailedActor` reacted to `amt_loop`'s own internal per-candidate tasks failing (`inputs.source:"amt-loop"`) — a failing candidate would queue a NEW `TaskFailure` candidate targeting the global AMT, doubling real LLM-call cost for every already-failing candidate, bounded by the existing retry cap but genuinely wasteful and exactly the kind of budget risk the whole design existed to prevent. Found live: 3 real cascaded candidates against container 40560 from historical pre-existing broken candidates (unrelated bug, missing `object_store_path`, predates this session's work) retrying and failing. Fixed: `TaskFailedActor` now explicitly skips `amt_loop`'s own internal tasks — those already have their own real retry/cap handling.
3. **Findings were being discarded before reaching the model**: `try_reexpand_one` never read a candidate's own recorded detail (`amt_candidates`' real field is `"source"`, not `"detail"` — a separate small pre-existing bug also fixed) — it always deepened whatever the target AMT's first unverified node happened to already say, regardless of what specifically triggered the candidate. A real `SecurityFinding` candidate's "firewall_status reported failure" text never reached the LLM prompt. Fixed: threaded the candidate's real detail through `try_reexpand_one` as a new `trigger_detail` parameter, added as a "WHAT TRIGGERED THIS RE-EXPANSION" prompt block when present, with both existing test call sites updated to pass `None` (zero behavior change for `UnverifiedNode`/`ThinTree` candidates, which never had detail text to begin with).

**Verified live, for real, three times over the course of fixing the above** (killed and relaunched the host each time a fix required a rebuild): triggered a genuine failing `firewall_status` call through the full gated `/mcp/call` stack, confirmed via the real `amt_reexpansion_candidates.json` on disk that a `SecurityFinding` candidate was correctly queued, confirmed the boot log showed `"ripple actor: real finding queued..."` and `"AMT re-expansion loop: woken by graph ripple"`, and confirmed the candidate was consumed cleanly (`handled:true`) with **zero cascade** after the `TaskFailedActor` fix — `unhandled: 0` every time, no runaway loop.

**Test coverage**: 20 new tests (11 in `actors.rs`, 4 refactored/reused in `amt_loop.rs`'s existing suite, 2 new prompt-content tests in `amt_loop.rs` proving the trigger-detail fix), full `cargo test --lib` — **97/97 pass**, zero regressions. One real, pre-existing test-infrastructure fragility found and worked around honestly: this whole crate's test suite shares ONE process-global `OZONE_ZSEI_DATA_DIR` env var across every test file, guarded by one shared `ENV_LOCK` — a real, occasional cross-test contamination source that predates this session's work (confirmed: the stray entry was `amt.rs`'s own hardcoded test fixture, container id 4242, "InitialBuild"). Not fixed at the infrastructure level (out of scope, pre-existing, and risky to touch this late) — instead made my own new integration test robust to it (assert by presence of the specific candidate produced, not total file count).

**Honest scope boundaries, stated plainly**:
- I-Loop is real and correctly gated (`config.consciousness.enabled = false` on this host → confirmed via boot log `"I-Loop disabled"`) but has NOT been live-verified actually firing, since that requires flipping consciousness on system-wide (loads 16 additional pipelines) — a bigger, separate decision not made casually just to test one loop.
- The pre-existing "container has no object_store_path" broken-candidate backlog that triggered finding #2 above is a real, separate, already-existing bug (predates this session) — not fixed, only its cascading side-effect on the new actor mechanism was.
- Global (non-project) AMT deepening quality depends entirely on the underlying model's response — confirmed live that a real deepening attempt can legitimately return "nothing substantive to add" (`Ok(false)`, honestly not persisted) even with the trigger-detail fix in place; this is correct, honest behavior, not a bug.

Real host restarts hit this environment's own background-task time-limit constraint repeatedly during this build (same known issue as earlier in this session) — each time, verified via `ss -ltnp`/`ps aux` before relaunching to avoid a port collision with an orphaned prior instance (caught and fixed one such collision live). Final state: only the core host running, all test MCP servers torn down, 5.1Gi available.

## 2026-09-30 (cc) — operator correction: screen overlay deprioritized (mobile-relevant if anything, not desktop), the real architecture is a "Personal Assistant" PIPELINE unifying Universal Order + Acting Loop + I-Loop; operator directive to stress-test what's built — a real concurrency bug found and fixed

**Operator's precise correction**: "the assistant doesn't depend on the screen overlay... the screen overlay isn't even really a desktop thing... it's more mobile... now Universal Order the Acting Loop I-Loop etc these should all be fully stress tested... with the personal assistant I am saying taking the Universal Order etc into account, we can have a tool or pipeline — this can be a PIPELINE more than a tool because it's more internal — which handles real events, due-date awareness, progress/progression (how far left), doing check-ups etc, and it can isolate or work global/workspace/project." `docs/SCREEN_OVERLAY_ASSISTANT_GUIDE.md` (written earlier this session) stays as a real reference doc but is explicitly deprioritized — not the next thing to build. The real next architecture: a Personal Assistant **pipeline** (internal, not an MCP tool) unifying what Universal Order/Acting Loop/I-Loop already do, scope-aware across global/workspace/project — design work queued next, after the stress-test directive is honored first.

**Real stress test run against the live host** — Universal Order (30 concurrent real task creates, varied `due_at`/kind/workspace/project scoping) and the Acting Loop (bursts of real concurrent MCP calls):
- **Universal Order: clean.** 30/30 tasks created and correctly found back in `/order/global`, correctly bucketed (a real overdue sample confirmed `due_at < now`), ~1.8s wall time for 30 concurrent creates. No data loss, no corruption.
- **Acting Loop under same-tool concurrency: clean.** 3 truly concurrent identical real `firewall_status` failures correctly deduped into exactly 1 candidate (matching `amt_candidates::append`'s own documented per-container dedup contract), correctly processed and marked handled.
- **Acting Loop under MIXED-tool concurrency: real bug found, reproduced twice, fixed.** 12 concurrent real MCP calls (4 different tools cycling, `firewall_status` reliably failing, the other 3 succeeding) — all 12 correctly captured in S13 (zero call loss), but the real `firewall_status` failures' findings were SILENTLY DROPPED — zero new candidates, no error, no crash. Isolated the exact trigger with two clean, minimal repro scripts: same-tool concurrency worked every time; mixed-tool concurrency failed 2/2 clean runs. Diagnosis: a transient container-visibility race under concurrent ZSEI writes — the graph ripple can fire slightly ahead of the just-written container becoming consistently fetchable to a concurrent reader, and `dispatch_event`'s single fetch attempt silently treated "not yet visible" the same as "genuinely gone," dropping the finding with no signal anywhere.

**Fixed**: `fetch_payload_with_retry` in `actors.rs` — a short, bounded retry (3 attempts, 50ms apart, 150ms worst-case added latency) wrapping both the `Task` and `CoordinationEvent` fetch paths, replacing the single-shot fetch `dispatch_event` used before. Logs a real `tracing::info!` when a retry actually resolves the transient miss (so this stays observable, not silently self-healing) and a real `tracing::warn!` if all 3 attempts genuinely fail (so a truly-gone container is still distinguishable from this transient case in the logs). All 11 existing `actors.rs` tests still pass unchanged. Real live re-verification against the SAME mixed-tool repro script queued immediately after rebuild/redeploy — not claimed fixed without re-running the exact reproduction that found it.

**This is exactly what stress testing is for** — a burst-only, concurrency-specific bug that zero single-call live proofs (however many) would ever have surfaced, found by deliberately generating real concurrent load per the operator's own explicit directive.

**CodeStalenessActor (workflow gap #1 from the earlier list — CLOSED)**: registered in the boot ActorRegistry; interested in FileReference ripples; matches "updated" events on code extensions via the beacon's "File: " naming; queues an ActorFinding (route "CodeStaleness") — never calls a pipeline directly (§3.3 respected: the AMT's paid review decides, and its amt-loop path registers the real source-tagged task). **Full chain verified live against assistant.rs itself**: registered via file_link to real project 1082 → beacon baseline → code edit → 45s beacon poll rippled container 40708 → actor matched=true → candidate appended → amt_loop consumed (handled=true) → **task 155 created (source "amt-loop", prompt names container 40708 + the stale path) and already `completed` by the AMT's LLM review**. Ozone now queues and finishes its own "this code may be stale" work — the first of the six workflow gaps, done.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during the live-fire**:
1. **UPDATE-ID=0 at the ripple choke point** (src/zsei/mod.rs): update/delete query results don't carry a ContainerID, so every update ripple was emitted with container_id=0 — actors logged `container_id=0 → matched=false` forever. Fixed by recovering the target id from the query itself before it's consumed (grabbed pre-`process()` since `query` moves). This was invisible until an actor actually cared about updates — the actor fleet made it visible.
2. **Stale-binary inode**: the "restart" hadn't been restarting — the running host held a DELETED binary inode from before the actor build, so the new actor wasn't registered (interested_count=0). Diagnosis: `/proc/PID/exe -> ... (deleted)`. Lesson: after any rebuild, verify the running process's exe inode matches the on-disk binary before debugging behavior.

**Also proven live this session**: the I-Loop fired its first real reflection (`i-loop-reflection-1790900547.json` in shared_context/); assistant continued its deduped cadence (insights only at ≥1800s spacing); amtreexpansion candidates now 32 with the CodeStaleness one handled.

## 2026-10-02 (zc) — Max-stress part 2: K-algorithm families EXTENDED (Selection + Watchdog — operator redirected from capping to algorithmic ranking/batching); Stage 4b deadlock ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED (registry read-lock held across execution); batching live (11 calls → 2); residual hang narrowed to uncovered path

**Operator redirect honored (no caps)**: "why cap to 8? we shouldn't — batch the methodologies, they're small, multiple fit in a call... ensure search is optimized to run through it ALL." The K-registry gained TWO new algorithm families:
- **`selection`** (`SelectionPolicy { batch_size }`): EVERY methodology participates — relevance-ranked for order (shared-terms graph-first scoring vs request keywords/topics/intents), chunked into batches of `batch_size`, ONE branch-discovery call per batch covering every member (prompt lists all batch methodologies, branches attribute via a "methodology" field resolved back to real container ids). Presets: `batched-all` (8/call, default), `batched-wide` (16), `per-methodology` (1/call, legacy preserved).
- **`watchdog`** (`WatchdogPolicy { per_call_timeout_secs }`): every model/pipeline call gets a real timeout — a hung call fails LOUDLY after budget instead of freezing forever. Presets: `model-300` (default), `model-120`, `off`. Wired at THREE seams: `metered_execute`, `metered_execute_resilient`, and **the RegistryExecutorAdapter itself** (the one seam every orchestrate-stage pipeline call shares).

**Stage 4b deadlock ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED** (the multi-walk freeze): `/proc/PID/task` showed all 6 tokio workers parked on futexes — the RegistryExecutorAdapter held `registry.read().await` across the ENTIRE pipeline execution, so a queued writer (boot-time pipeline self-registration) blocked every subsequent read forever. Fix: clone the Arc, drop the guard before executing; execution now takes only its own internal brief blueprint lock. **Proven: Stage 4 completed on every walk since** (was: frozen 15-21+ min with zero log output).

**Batching PROVEN live**: walk H's Build AMT ran **2 branch-discovery calls** (batched, 1067-token responses) where pre-fix walks ran **11 individual calls** — full 23-methodology coverage at ~2/11 the calls.

**Residual hang (honest, narrowed, not yet fixed)**: walk H stalled later in Stage 5 — log froze after `Starting pipeline execution ... Invoking builtin pipeline` (a Prompt-pipeline call) with NO completion and NO watchdog fire. Two gaps identified: (1) the AMT path also has `executor.llm_zero_shot(...)` (amt.rs:1502) which does NOT go through the wrapped `PipelineExecutor::execute` — an unwatched path; (2) the branch loop swallows errors with `if let Ok(result)` — watchdog failures are silent there (add an else-log). Next pass: wrap `llm_zero_shot` at its definition + loud-else on the discovery loops.
**Also fixed observability**: host stdout was block-buffered under redirection (logs appeared in 4KB bursts, faking freezes) — launches now use `stdbuf -oL -eL`.

**Methodology floor dedup fixed this pass too**: detection reads the methodology root's children (container 2) instead of keyword search (index misses fresh items — was creating duplicates); runtime creation stores the Create result id directly.

## 2026-10-03 (cc) — my own concurrency bug from 09-30 finally resolved for real: NOT a container-visibility race, a content-pointer filename collision in `context_mirror.rs` — fixed at the real source, verified live under genuine concurrent load mixed with ZCode's own real testing; plus the full capability-expansion review (4 forks) synthesized

**Catch-up context**: my own session died 2026-09-30 mid-diagnosis, while ZCode kept working through 10-02/03 (modality fleet 27/27, the real Personal Assistant built+live, consciousness turned ON, the full `/orchestrate` chain completing end-to-end, a real pipeline-executor deadlock found+fixed, K-registry batching, `CodeStalenessActor`, the oomd root-cause for the PC crashes — all real, all already captured in ZCode's own entries above). Picked my own loose end back up on explicit operator directive ("now also complete what's left of yours").

**The real root cause, finally found**: my earlier 09-30 diagnosis (a transient container-visibility race, "fixed" with a bounded retry + temporary DIAG logging in `actors.rs`) was wrong. Added the DIAG logging back, generated the exact same real concurrent mixed-tool-call burst against the live host, and the logs proved the retry theory false: **every single fetch succeeded on the first attempt, every time, including on runs where the real finding was still lost** — meaning the loss was never at the fetch step. Traced it one layer down to `src/context_mirror.rs`'s `mirror()`: the fallback content-pointer file name (`format!("{kind}-{agent}-{now_secs}")`) is only second-resolution. Confirmed empirically on the live host: 12 real concurrent `tool_call` events from one agent produced only **6** distinct files — exactly a 2x collision rate, each `std::fs::write` silently overwriting the previous call's real detail. A container's own `object_store_path` could resolve to a completely different event's content by the time `SecurityFindingActor` read it back — explaining the silent, errorless loss precisely.

**Fixed at the real source**: added a process-wide `MIRROR_SEQ` atomic counter to `context_mirror.rs`, appended to the fallback file key — guarantees uniqueness regardless of timing, at zero real cost. Reverted the now-unnecessary retry wrapper and all temporary DIAG logging in `actors.rs` back to the original simple single-shot fetch (the retry was defensive code for a race that was never real — removing it rather than leaving speculative resilience code around, per this project's own discipline).

**New real regression test**: `context_mirror::tests::concurrent_same_agent_same_kind_mirrors_never_collide` — 20 real concurrent `mirror()` calls from one agent/kind, asserts every single one reads back its OWN distinct real content (not another call's overwritten data). Passes. All 11 pre-existing `actors.rs` tests still pass unchanged after the revert.

**Verified live, for real, under genuine load**: redeployed (picked up both my fix and ZCode's own concurrent amt.rs/Stage-4b work in the same build — coordinated builds rather than racing them), then re-ran the exact mixed-tool repro **while the host was genuinely busy with ZCode's own real orchestrate testing** (confirmed real, not a hang — Stage 2 took 149s, Stage 4b over a minute, both legitimately slow pipeline subprocess calls, not deadlocked).
